## BL-804 — a remediator writes `derivations: not replayed` although replay is its default, and the join's refusal costs a whole re-dispatch

Closed in 0.949.0 on a tree receipt, reopened by BL-817. Its decision rule is the step-value ruling, "A step with no measured value goes" (`.claude/rules/operator-rulings.md`).

Closed in 0.949.0 (verified 94c8dcdc7) on a tree receipt, reopened by BL-817.

**DEFECT (efficiency).** Filed at the batch-242 close from the graph watch (ai-dlc-fa, poll 16, 01:07 EDT 2026-10-26). In graph s320, the `stories-party --pass 1` repair join refused at 05:05:46Z with `REFUSED: 11.md: derivations: not replayed; …` (the checks-field refusal in `core/scripts/join-remediator-shards.sh`). Part 11 is story 3.5, in `s320/shards/stories-party-repair-p1/11.md`. `core/team-roles/remediator.md:134-139` makes replay the default ("Replay is the default: run `scripts/ai-dlc/validate-artifact-derivations.sh <your record>` after your last edit"), but the remediator wrote `not replayed` anyway. Graph's lead recovered by contract: a record-only re-run (agent a0481edc5f18691e2) replayed 5 derivations and re-marked, and the re-join returned rc=0. The cost was one dispatch and about 2 minutes. One instance, measured from the transcript.

Owed: a mechanism that makes the default the act, not the instruction. For example, the `--mark` path of `wait-for-deliverable.sh` could run the replay itself when a repair record says `not replayed` and its fences name no deferred document, then write the count. That moves a refusal the join already makes to the point where the remediator can still fix it. Measure the false-positive set first: a record whose fences are legitimately deferred must not be forced.

Former receipt (the mechanism, not the outcome): `core/fixtures/remediator-shard-join/receipt-bl804.sh`, removed under BL-915 as unreferenced, since no runner invoked it.

Re-audit (BL-817): graph s320, `docs/analysis/pipeline-efficiency-s317-s320/scripts/` (extract.py, roll.py as committed at fc71a7b10 on main; the step rows of steps.csv per story); per story, stories-test-strategy: 0.93 h gap-inclusive, token-equivalent $39.1 (API $39.1), 65.0M tokens, the baseline. No graph sprint has run 0.949.0 yet. Owed: first graph sprint on >=0.949.0. The shipped mechanism (0.949.0, `wait-for-deliverable.sh --mark` replays the derivations) stands; the reopen owes the outcome, not a rebuild.

Owed (batch b244, c4), the premise of the mechanism above corrected. The ordering was not the defect: `core/team-roles/remediator.md` orders `join-remediator-shards.sh --escalations` before `wait-for-deliverable.sh --mark-repair` on purpose, `core/skills/ai-dlc/steps/_gate-procedures.md` agrees, and graph's repair subagent (agent-a2ba43cd07934d6e7, 2026-10-27T09:01Z) followed it. Its join refused `derivations: not replayed`; it replayed, edited the line to `replayed 1`, re-joined at rc 0, and then marked. That cost about 16s inside the dispatch and no re-dispatch, so the join before the mark is the designed loop. The real defect was one sentence in `remediator.md` that said `--mark` and `--mark-repair` replay the record themselves, which offered the serial or unsharded remediator `not replayed` as an option its own earlier join refuses. Resolved: the sentence now scopes the replay to `--mark` and says the serial remediator replays before its join and writes `replayed <n>` itself, pinned in `process-rule-pins` by the `rem-serial-replay` row, a named mutant that restores the old clause, and the `rem-serial-optout` arm. The entry stays OPEN: it is decided by its receipt plus a measured outcome under BL-817, the first graph sprint on a release carrying this sentence showing no `not replayed` refusal at the join.

**Progress (BL-804, batch b250, 2026-11-05: the close predicate is a DEAD-PREMISE).** The predicate in the paragraph above, "no `not replayed` refusal at the join", cannot be met by a correct engine. The refusal is correct whenever derivation fences go stale. Separately, `--mark-repair` already runs the replay (`core/scripts/wait-for-deliverable.sh:650-665`, term 6), and `core/fixtures/remediator-shard-join` arm EC41 asserts it (`run.sh:230-231`). On graph s321 (subagent agent-aa2b18d38429a92ba, 2026-11-04 11:13:37Z) the refusal came from 21 stale fences inside the sealed block. The agent fixed them in the same dispatch, the join returned rc=0 at 11:16:18Z, and there was no re-dispatch.

The question is re-keyed: does a `not replayed` refusal cost a re-dispatch. Measured over s321 (707 graph transcripts, from extract.py's s320/s321 edge 2026-10-30T00:10:03Z to graph e32f9ce2f), with patterns keyed on the emitters' text at `core/scripts/join-remediator-shards.sh:1684` and `core/scripts/wait-for-deliverable.sh:914`. The controls in the same run were 896 join calls, 84 join REFUSED results and 93 serial-remediator spawns, with an impossible token at 0. After the fix reached graph (reconcile #1271, merged 2026-10-28T01:36Z, 0.958.0) there were 3 refusals (one in s321, two in s320 under 0.969.0), all fixed in-dispatch, and 0 re-dispatches. Before it, 1 of 5 refusals cost a re-dispatch (11.md, 2026-10-26, under 0.948.0).

The entry does not close on this. At the before-rate, 3 refusals predict 0.6 re-dispatches, which is below 1, so the zero cannot discriminate. The Baseline (stories-test-strategy per-story cost) is UNMEASURABLE on s321 because that step is incomplete. The close condition is now re-dispatches per refusal, measured once enough refusals have accrued that the before-rate predicts at least one. The entry stays LIVE and PARTIAL, with `verify: manual`.

NOTE: on s321 the join accepted an unearned `replayed 147` at 11:15:11Z while the validator still failed, and `--mark-repair`'s own replay refused it at 11:15:19Z. The cost was one extra in-dispatch refusal and no re-dispatch. The join's checks field trusts the stated count, and `--mark-repair` is the backstop.

**Progress (batch 251).** The join verifier that NOTE suggests is NOT built: a contract adversary found it needs none, and the result is NO CHANGE. `--mark` on a remediator part already checks a claimed `replayed <n>` (`core/scripts/wait-for-deliverable.sh:791-792`, `:848` and `:889-913`), and fixture arm EC42 (`core/fixtures/remediator-shard-join/run.sh:274-276`) drives it through cases a2 and a3 (`core/fixtures/remediator-shard-join/lib.sh:1424-1441`). `--mark-repair` checks the segment it closes (`wait-for-deliverable.sh:666-670`, BL-816). An unmarked record reads unfinished at the beat (`has_marker`, `wait-for-deliverable.sh:550`), and the join refuses a part or record whose bytes differ from its seal (`core/scripts/join-remediator-shards.sh:2016-2026`, `:2255-2259`), so an unchecked count cannot reach the join through either path. The one path left is a stale fence in a shard segment, which `--mark-repair` leaves to the join; it is backstopped by the lead's `validate-artifact-derivations.sh <joined record>` (`core/skills/ai-dlc/steps/_gate-procedures.md:1340`, with exit 1 returning the record at `:1377`). The s321 incident cost 8 s inside one dispatch and no re-dispatch. The check site is therefore `--mark`/`--mark-repair`, the call every subject already passes through; a join copy would be a second site for one check (`.claude/rules/mechanism-design.md`, "Site the duty where the declaration decides"). BL-804 stays PARTIAL on its refusal-accrual predicate (re-dispatches per refusal, measured once the before-rate predicts at least one), with `verify: manual` kept.

**Progress (2026-11-11, batch b255 F4: the refusal count has not accrued, the entry stays PARTIAL).** No `Outcome measured:` line is written, because the count is below the owed 5. Post-fix is defined by when graph INSTALLED the sentence in `remediator.md`: graph commit `3bd62256f` (reconcile #1271, 0.954.0 to 0.958.0, merged 2026-10-27T21:36:45-04:00 = 2026-10-28T01:36:45Z), the first commit whose `.claude/team-roles/remediator.md` carries both "replays a part or shard record itself" and "replays before its join, which runs before"; `929c8a61c` (0.951.0, 2026-10-26T22:23-04:00) lacks both. The sweep is every graph transcript under `~/.claude/projects/-Users-n8-git-graph` (6176 files), keyed on the emitter text of graph's INSTALLED `scripts/ai-dlc/join-remediator-shards.sh` (`refuse "${b}: ${w}; a remediator's record states under its sha triple ..."`, with `${w}` reading `derivations: not replayed`, optionally followed by a parenthesis such as `(the unterminated block from line 1411)`), present unchanged in every installed copy from `1da426ef9` through `768cfa059`, and on the `wait-for-deliverable.sh` text `states 'derivations: not replayed', and the replay this call ran failed` (installed from `72e1cd749`, and from `929c8a61c` on a branch). Result: N = 3 post-fix refusals, all of them the three already counted under batch b250 (2026-10-29T15:37:36Z and 2026-10-29T16:30:13Z in s320; 2026-11-04T11:13:37Z in s321), each fixed inside its own dispatch (the subagent transcripts end 42 s, 18 s and 177 s later) and 0 re-dispatches. The s321 remainder (after graph e32f9ce2f, 2026-11-05T00:26Z, to the s322 edge 2026-11-09T00:00Z) and s322 (to 2026-11-11T19:56Z) add 0 refusals. The `wait-for-deliverable.sh` key matched 0 over the whole corpus, so no `--mark` or `--mark-repair` replay refusal has occurred in a graph transcript at all. ## BL-695 — a sharded stories party round waits on its slowest seat at every wave, because the lead split the round by seat and joined each seat before dispatching the next

Closed in 0.950.0 on a mechanism measurement; step cost did not fall; reopened by BL-817. Its decision rule is the step-value ruling, "A step with no measured value goes" (`.claude/rules/operator-rulings.md`).

**DEFECT (efficiency).** f7 measured, graph s319 lead sessions c61a91eb and a0701ce0, 0.830.0; transcript-only, not re-derived here: (1) in the stories-test-strategy party round, per-story seats ran a median of 5.5-6.5 min and cross seats a median of 15.5-18.3 min, with one cross seat at 43.2 min; (2) f7 estimated the pole at 40-80 min per round — an estimate, not a measurement; (3) the TEA seat ran per-story n=19, median 2.0 min, max 6.6, and cross n=6, median 17.5 min, max 35.3, about 20-29 min of pole per wave. The batch 236 WAVES adversary read the same two transcripts, read only: "Concurrent subagent limit reached" appears 0 times in either (control: 9 other graph transcripts carry it); the round went out as four waves of 25 agents, one seat each (19 per-story + 6 cross), against a probable cap of 42, so about 17 slots sat idle in every wave. From each wave's last seat to the next dispatch: Dev to Architect 35.6 min after the last per-story seat and 15.6 min after the last cross seat (beat cadence plus a 429 episode); Architect to TEA 11.8 min and 14 s; TEA ended the round on a 29.1 min pole. The cross pole in each in-session transition is the gap between its last per-story seat and its last cross seat: (35.6 - 15.6) + (11.8 - 0.2) = 20.0 + 11.6 = 31.6 min across the two. The 15.6 min of beat cadence and 429 overhead after the Dev round's last cross seat is separate from that pole. SM to Dev is excluded: it was a session handoff.

Cause: a per-seat barrier the LEAD imposed, with the cap never probed — not one the cap forced. The lead wrote "the other three seats follow once the harness cap is known from any rejections", against `rule-28.md`'s "until a rejection has shown the cap, the first wave is the whole set". Two prose sites licensed the barrier: Rule 28's Waves paragraph joined each wave "before the next wave spawns", and both Rule 28's missing-set sentence and `_gate-procedures.md`'s party-round join folded the rejected set into "the next wave". Nothing in a later wave reads a seat file, so the barrier buys nothing. This is the open measurement of BL-464 claim 7, which is the parent: the per-group cross fan-out shipped, and the pole that remains is the barrier.

Owed: the prose half lands in this release. `rule-28.md` takes the cap from `CLAUDE_CODE_MAX_CONCURRENT_SUBAGENTS` when the lead can read it, else from the rejection probe, never from a remembered number; sizes a wave at no less than min(remaining, cap - running); says a seat is not a wave; refills a slot on each exit; arms one beat per refill over only that refill's paths, never `--reset`; adds the undispatched-members exit as a third uncovered notification; and re-dispatches MISSING after the join and the rejected set on the first exit. `_gate-procedures.md` splits MISSING from rejected and dispatches cross members first, worded as a tendency (the earlier calls were admitted first in 52 of 63 graph messages with rejections, and not in 11). The detector is the read-only transcript-replay script `scripts/audit-party-wave-barrier.sh`, which judges a delivered exit of a round member whose response turn carries no Agent/Task dispatch for the round while RUNNING < CAP; no beat is read, and the earlier beat predicate and its narrowings are gone. Its false-positive set is measured on the graph transcripts plus c61a91eb and a0701ce0. Its remaining blind spots: (1) a ceiling below the harness cap that someone legitimately set, because the replay reads no conversation; (2) operator pause or question windows, which read as unanswered exits: two of the 13 corpus findings over 187 graph transcripts, 22e0f5c2 b30 and ea675b67 b32, sit wholly inside a Rule 29 pause and are FALSE (the other 11 are TRUE); (3) an enqueue with no delivery is never judged; (4) RUNNING undercounts when Agent records are out of the harness's order, measured up to 16 mid-wave, so an idle count of 1-3 is within that error; (5) a round re-run under the same name reads as a later batch; (6) a round's last stretch, with no later new member, is never judged; (7) with no readable cap and no `--cap`, the transcript is unjudged (UNKNOWN-CAP). A live PreToolUse WARN is not buildable, because the dispatch-guard matcher is `Agent|Task` only, so it cannot see the Bash beat, and it cannot know whether round members remain undispatched. The real close is the consumer's next stories round showing seats refilling during the cross pole.

Lead ruling: s319 (a0701ce0) was a real barrier, but the detector reads it UNKNOWN-CAP because the transcript records no cap. With `--cap 42` it fires twice (batches 2 and 3, running=24, idle=18, gaps 3348 s and 1364 s). The Rule 28 cap order, the environment variable when readable and otherwise the rejection probe, is what puts the cap into future transcripts.

Receipt: the detector run as a transcript replay, plus the prose conjuncts. It exits 9 if `scripts/audit-party-wave-barrier.sh`, `rule-28.md`, `_gate-procedures.md` or `_dispatch-protocol.md` is missing, then writes seeds (a), (b), (c) and (e) into a `mktemp` dir it leaves in place, and requires the detector to exit 0 on (b), refill on exit, and on (c), a cap-forced wave, and to exit 1 on (a), a wave held for the previous join with slots free, and on (e), the measured s319 shape: four 25-member seat waves, each fully joined, cap 42 from a `CLAUDE_CODE_MAX_CONCURRENT_SUBAGENTS=42` tool_result line, three FINDINGs at idle 18. Seed (c) is what kills the free-slot test removed: without it that mutant only widens findings and the three other seeds cannot see it. The prose is read with newlines folded to spaces, since both files wrap. "A rejected spawn is undelivered" must be in `rule-28.md` (a positive control, else exit 9); "next wave carries" must be absent from `rule-28.md` and "the next wave carries" from `_gate-procedures.md`; `join each wave|wave is joined|before the next wave spawns` must be absent from both, and "Each wave is joined in ONE beat" or "wave is joined" from `_dispatch-protocol.md`; `CLAUDE_CODE_MAX_CONCURRENT_SUBAGENTS` and, in any case, "a seat is not a wave" must be present in `rule-28.md`. Anything else is exit 1.

**Progress (0.857.0; entry stays OPEN).** Shipped: the prose half and the detector.
- Prose: `rule-28.md`, `_gate-procedures.md` and `_dispatch-protocol.md` size a wave from the harness cap, refill it on each exit, and no longer treat a seat as a wave.
- Detector: `scripts/audit-party-wave-barrier.sh`, a read-only transcript replay, with the `party-wave-barrier` fixture.
- Corpus census, from the detector header's FINAL STATE: 13 findings over 187 graph transcripts, 11 TRUE and 2 FALSE. The two FALSE are 22e0f5c2 b30 and ea675b67 b32, both inside a Rule 29 pause window, which the replay cannot see (residual 2).
- The entry stays open until the consumer's next stories round is measured.

**The shipped-half receipt (batch 240; quoted, not installed as `verify:`).** It exits 0 on origin/main at 0.924.0, because the prose half and the detector shipped, so as the closing line it read the entry as a CLOSE-CANDIDATE while the consumer half is still owed: `D=scripts/audit-party-wave-barrier.sh; R=core/skills/ai-dlc/rule-bodies/rule-28.md; G=core/skills/ai-dlc/steps/_gate-procedures.md; P=core/skills/ai-dlc/steps/_dispatch-protocol.md; [ -f "$D" ] || exit 9; [ -f "$R" ] && [ -f "$G" ] && [ -f "$P" ] || exit 9; T="$(mktemp -d "${TMPDIR:-/tmp}/bl695.XXXXXX")" || exit 9; bash "$D" --seed a "$T/a.jsonl" --seed b "$T/b.jsonl" --seed c "$T/c.jsonl" --seed e "$T/e.jsonl" || exit 9; bash "$D" "$T/b.jsonl" >/dev/null 2>&1; rb=$?; bash "$D" "$T/c.jsonl" >/dev/null 2>&1; rc=$?; bash "$D" "$T/a.jsonl" >/dev/null 2>&1; ra=$?; bash "$D" "$T/e.jsonl" >/dev/null 2>&1; re=$?; [ "$rb" = 0 ] && [ "$rc" = 0 ] && [ "$ra" = 1 ] && [ "$re" = 1 ] || exit 1; r="$(tr '\n' ' ' < "$R" | tr -s ' ')"; g="$(tr '\n' ' ' < "$G" | tr -s ' ')"; p="$(tr '\n' ' ' < "$P" | tr -s ' ')"; n="$(grep -c 'A rejected spawn is undelivered' <<<"$r")" || n=0; [ "$n" -gt 0 ] || exit 9; n1="$(grep -c 'next wave carries' <<<"$r")" || n1=0; n2="$(grep -c 'the next wave carries' <<<"$g")" || n2=0; n3="$(grep -cE 'join each wave|wave is joined|before the next wave spawns' <<<"$r")" || n3=0; n4="$(grep -cE 'join each wave|wave is joined|before the next wave spawns' <<<"$g")" || n4=0; n5="$(grep -c 'CLAUDE_CODE_MAX_CONCURRENT_SUBAGENTS' <<<"$r")" || n5=0; n6="$(grep -ci 'a seat is not a wave' <<<"$r")" || n6=0; n7="$(grep -cE 'Each wave is joined in ONE beat|wave is joined' <<<"$p")" || n7=0; [ "$n1" = 0 ] && [ "$n2" = 0 ] && [ "$n3" = 0 ] && [ "$n4" = 0 ] && [ "$n7" = 0 ] && [ "$n5" -gt 0 ] && [ "$n6" -gt 0 ] || exit 1; exit 0`

## BL-868 — no fixture verdict or invocation count may depend on load: the `±` rows, the polls and the watchdogs go

**DEFECT.** Operator ruling, 2026-10-28 18:34 EDT: "There should be ZERO tests that depend on load. If they exist, they are defects." The umbrella: a fixture whose verdict or invocation count depends on scheduling or load is a defect. The end state has three parts. First, no `invocations.decl` carries a `±` row. Second, the judge (`scripts/validate-fixture-invocations.sh`) refuses the `±` form at J4, with a self-probe that fires on a seeded `±` row. Removing the `±` form also deletes its `why=<the loop that retries or polls> provisional` grammar from the judge; `scripts/validate-fixture-hygiene.sh` (H5) parses `why=` only on its `MUTANT-MATRIX` rows, so it carries no `±` grammar to delete. Third, no fixture verdict is decided by a wall-clock or elapsed-poll watchdog.

**Known instances, each with its owner session and state.**

1. `readset-skip`, `readset-skip-b` and `readset-skip-c` `±` rows. These are the deriver's sentinel-settle and end-flush polls, and `-b`'s REAL CANARY bounded retries. Owner: ai-dlc-0d (p1). In progress.
2. `snapshot-archive-rotate` `±` row: the kill-point sweep workers and their stop file. Owner: ai-dlc-a2.
3. `wait-beat-liveness` `±` rows: the beat poll loop (one `--check-header` per poll) and the gated sleep stub. Owner: ai-dlc-a1. CLOSED (v0.969.0, batch b245): its `invocations.decl` carries no `±` row.
4. `readset-skip` CK MUTANT fileentries. Under load, the pre-push pool's no-progress watchdog REFUSED a 0-second unit in world (c) push 1, which moved the vector. Seen in the 0.963.0 warm run at load about 119. Owner: ai-dlc-0d. In progress.
5. BL-867: counted scripts piped into early-exit readers (`awk` exit, `head`, `grep -m`, `grep -q`). SIGPIPE makes the number of bash starts scheduling-dependent. Owner: ai-dlc-0d. `self-update-gate` was fixed by ai-dlc-e2 in 0.963.0.
6. Removal of the provisional `±` judge form, after items 1 to 3 land. Owner: ai-dlc-0d.
8. Real-time waits still in fixtures (filed 2026-10-30; the conversion side is owned by BL-878 item 5, the injected clock applied as part of each fixture's conversion; BL-868 keeps the `±` judge-form removal (item 6) and its own receipt). Each one is wall clock that load can stretch, and none carries a `±` row, so the receipt below cannot see them:
   - postcompact-rulebook-recovery `run.sh:2068` (`sleep 16`) and `:2101` (`sleep 3`): late transcript writers.

   The lever is the one wait-beat-liveness took (item 3): an injected clock, or a poll hook triggered by the subject's own sleep. Where a real process is the point, block on a sentinel the subject writes, never on elapsed time. The sites were found by a source grep, `(^|[^a-z_])sleep [0-9.]+|exec sleep`, with comment lines excluded. Whether each one is a race the arm needs, or only pacing, was not judged.

10. `readset-skip`'s counted start count moves with timing: one counted run measured 88 against the declared 87 (`core/scripts/derive-fixture-readsets.sh`'s row has read 87 in every revision; `git log -S'88 core/scripts/derive-fixture-readsets.sh'` on its `invocations.decl` returns no commit, against 2 for `-S'87 …'`). The judge's own B8 header lists that fixture as backgrounding and killing a bash and using `timeout`. Owed under this ruling; not fixed in batch b247.

(hdiutil: create failed - Directory not empty)`, and rc 0 when run alone. The cause is unmeasured; two runs sharing one sandbox image path would produce this. 15. REMOVED by BL-915 (batch b250): operator ruling "Remove it." supersedes the b248 and 2026-11-03 rulings below. The `td-plain-amp` mutant, the drive's `TD_CEIL` ceiling and the bounded form of `td_more`/`td_hang` are gone from `core/fixtures/prepush-pool-depth/run.sh`, and `core/fixtures/fixture-hygiene/receipt-bl868.sh` no longer carries an exemption; the item is not owed. As filed: `prepush-pool-depth`'s `td-plain-amp` mutant scored on a 5-second observer ceiling (the drive's `TD_CEIL`, passed by this one mutant). Every other teardown wait in that fixture ends on an event and carries no bound. This mutant had no event to wait on. In the INT drive, the mutant starts xargs with a plain `&`, so SIGINT is ignored in every worker and the kernel discards the observer's `kill -INT` when it is sent: no trap runs, no record is cleared and nothing is written. In the TERM drive, xargs leads no group, so `pgrep -g "$pool_xp"` lists nothing. The only external call in `pool_stop`, that listing, runs BEFORE the per-record decision, and the loop then runs only builtins (`read`, `case`) into a `wait` that never returns. A marker on the listing would precede the decision, so reading "not signalled" after it would infer the outcome from text, not observe a signal. Operator ruling, batch b248: the arm stayed on its ceiling and was filed here as open. Operator ruling 2026-11-03: item 15 was exempt from closing (superseded by BL-915).

**Progress (batch b255, release F7).** Two flakes are fixed, and item 5 is resolved by census. The entry stays PARTIAL on items 8, 9, 10, 12 and 14 as the b251 paragraph states them.

- `review-shard-merge` and `review-shard-merge-b` (they share `core/fixtures/review-shard-merge/lib.sh`). Cause: the `G()` git wrapper set no maintenance config. Under hermetic-run's git (Apple 2.54.0: `readset_git_exec_path`, run from the hook's READSET_TOOLS span, resolves `/Library/Developer/CommandLineTools/usr/libexec/git-core`, the git `/usr/bin/git` dispatches to; Homebrew's is 2.56.0), every commit through `G` spawned a detached `git maintenance run --auto`. That child repacks loose objects while a following `cp -R` of the same repository walks `.git/objects` (`lib.sh:826`, `:3043`, `:3974`), so a fan-out directory vanishes mid-copy, at a rate set by load. `gc.auto=0` does not stop it. The archived BL-381 (`docs/backlog.archive.md:25932-25935`) measured the same packer on git 2.54 in `layer-adjudication-tier`: it is governed by `maintenance.auto`, and `-c gc.auto=0` packs exactly as the default does. Fix: `G()` (`lib.sh:218`) passes `-c maintenance.auto=false`. Arm GM1 (`p_gmaint`, `lib.sh:260`, in the new unit `gmaint` dealt to shard a) counts the trace2 `child_start` events whose argv opens `git maintenance run`. One commit through `G` must spawn 0. The same commit through raw git spawns 1 as the positive control, and on a git that has `git maintenance` a control reading anything else is FIXTURE BROKEN (exit 2), never a skip. All three drives run under `GIT_CONFIG_NOSYSTEM=1 GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL=/dev/null` (`gm_children`, `lib.sh:224`), so an inherited `maintenance.auto=false` in a system or global gitconfig cannot zero the control. On a git with no `maintenance` subcommand (before 2.29, where a commit spawns `gc --auto` instead), the control cannot read 1. There `gm_has_maint` (`lib.sh:238`) reads git's own unknown-command text, never the exit code, and `gm_vacuous` (`lib.sh:248`) believes an absent subcommand only from a git older than 2.29: it parses `git --version` and exits 2 (FIXTURE BROKEN) on 2.29 or later, or on a version it cannot parse. On an older git the unit prints a distinct `ok GM1 VACUOUS on git <v>` line that claims no spawn count (`run.sh:861-871`). Fire-proofs on the extracted shipped text: an always-absent `gm_has_maint` on Apple git 2.54.0 exits 2; a shim reporting 2.28.0 plus the unknown-command text prints the VACUOUS line, rc 0; one reporting 2.30.0 exits 2. A present subcommand's `-h` exits 129 under Apple git 2.54 and 0 under Homebrew 2.56, and an unknown one exits 1 under both. The mutant is `G`'s own `declare -f` body with the flag stripped, guarded by `cmp -s`, with the flag counted 1 in `G` and 0 in the mutant. It spawns 1, so the arm fails. The arm counts spawns, not repacks, so no load can move it. Row `gm-no-maint` in the MUTANT-MATRIX block.
- `handoff-completion-assertion`. Cause: `run.sh:773` ran `chmod +x` on the pinned-clock `date` shim, but `tools.decl` declared no `chmod`. In the farm-only PATH the chmod was `command not found`, the shim stayed non-executable, PATH lookup skipped it, and the real `date` answered. So the `(pb)` guard passed only when `HCA_EPOCH` and the guard fell in the same epoch second. Fix: `tools.decl` declares `chmod`, and the guard (`run.sh:777`) first asserts that PATH resolves `date` to the shim itself. Measured on the extracted block under a frozen clock and a ticking clock: at base, with no chmod, frozen read PASS and ticking read BROKEN. At the fix, with no chmod, both read BROKEN, and with chmod both read PASS. Through `hermetic-run.sh` with the declaration removed from a detached copy, 3 of 3 runs were FIXTURE BROKEN; with it declared, PASS. `invocations.decl` `*` 519 -> 574, because the shim now runs as bash.
- Item 5 is RESOLVED BY CENSUS. The figure "428 sites whose writer is a function" cannot be reproduced. It entered in e311bf7e4 (`git log -S'The 428 sites'` names that commit) with no grammar beside it. Re-census at cb6e0ab80 with a quote- and nesting-aware grammar, `python3 docs/analysis/bl868-item5-pipe-census.py <repo> cb6e0ab80` (stdlib only). It reads each fixture's `run.sh` and depth-1 `*.sh`, 438 files (with `--all`, every nested `*.sh`, 443 files, the same 693 and the same split), and finds 693 pipes into an early-exit reader (`awk` exit, `head`, `grep -m`, `grep -q`). Of those, 27 have a DIRECT bash writer (14 fixtures) and 28 a function writer that starts bash (FUNC-START, 5 fixtures). Another 42 (FUNC-NOSTART) and 596 (OTHER) start no counted bash upstream. A per-site reading of the 55 found 0 offenders: 5 parser false positives, 15 acquitted with measurement in b248 (the item 5 bullet above), 16 whose writer output is captured whole (`dcat`) before the reader, 13 whose subject starts no bash, and 6 safe by output order. That per-site split is the b255 diagnosis hand's reading; this release re-derived the 693/27/28 counts, not the split. A count-safety arm, deciding per pipe whether a start can be lost, cannot have an empty false-positive set: safety turns on the writer's output order, which the pipe's text does not carry. A shape-ban arm, refusing any counted writer piped into an early-exit reader, has an enumerated false-positive set: 26 count-bearing pipes in 13 fixtures, plus 21 wrapped sites. The shape-ban is an optional follow-up and is not owed by this item.

There is no graph pipeline step behind this entry: it is a test-determinism defect in this repo's fixture suite, and `docs/analysis/bl868-item5-pipe-census.py` is a census of fixture source, not a reading of a consumer sprint; the cost lives in core/fixtures/, as verdicts and invocation counts that move with load.

This entry owns the last row of `scripts/inert-receipt-allowlist.txt`, `core/fixtures/fixture-hygiene/receipt-bl868.sh` (re-attributed from BL-915 in 0.1019.0). BL-868's close wires that receipt into a runner or deletes it, and deletes the row in the same change. Wiring it into `fixture-hygiene` is the wrong home: the receipt reads `docs/backlog.md` and every fixture file, so that fixture would re-key on both.

## BL-878 — every fixture is converted to in-process unit cells, and no session de-scopes a conversion

**DEFECT.** Operator ruling, 2026-10-30: "those entries would not allow for future sessions to descope the conversions. We would move forward on everything, full stop." Every fixture under `core/fixtures/*/run.sh` is converted. No fixture is dropped, deferred or judged not worth converting; the scope changes only by an operator ruling.

**Population, derived.** The fixtures still to convert are the rows of `scripts/fixture-inprocess-allowlist.txt`, which BL-879 seeds once from the tree and which only shrinks. Never hand-list them here; read the allowlist.

**Batches.** Group the allowlisted fixtures by shared subject script: build the graph whose edges join a fixture to every script it runs, taken from its `invocations.decl` subject rows and from its calls into `core/scripts/`, `core/hooks/`, `core/skills/` and `scripts/`. A batch is one connected component of that graph, so two sessions never change the same script in parallel. One session owns a component until every fixture in it is converted. Order the components by the summed loaded seconds of their fixtures in `$(git rev-parse --git-common-dir)/ai-dlc-fixture-durations`, costliest first. The durations decide ORDER only, never membership.

**Done, per fixture.**

1. Every cell runs in process. The only command-line runs left are one per distinct CLI outcome of the subject, each with an equivalence cell proving the in-process answer is byte-identical to the command-line answer (stdout, stderr and exit code) on a refusing input and on a passing input that differ.
2. The subject is made loadable by a PURE MOVE behind `[ "${BASH_SOURCE[0]}" = "$0" ] || return 0`, verified by a sorted line-multiset diff showing only the guard and its comment, and by `bash -n`. Where a pure move cannot reach a decision, the larger change is made: embedded code ported to a module, a new shipped file packaged (`install.sh`, both manifests, `uninstall.sh`, per `.claude/rules/consumer-boundary.md`), a self path read from `BASH_SOURCE`. Each of these carries the same equivalence proof as item 1.
3. Mutant cells and the `--matrix` scoring are converted. Each mutant's scratch copy is sourced once, and only its owning cell or cells run against it. Every mutant is killed by the cell that owns its property, never only collaterally. A fixture with no `MUTANT-MATRIX` block gets a kill harness and a matrix written from it, and leaves `scripts/fixture-matrix-allowlist.txt` in the same change.
4. Seeded worlds are built once and copied per cell (clause 4 of `.claude/rules/fixture-mutants.md`).
5. A fixture that sleeps or polls gets the injected clock as part of its conversion; the sites and the lever are BL-868 item 8, which stays the owning entry.
6. `invocations.decl` is re-measured by a counted hermetic run, and the read set is re-traced with the sandbox tracer.
7. A cell stays end to end ONLY where a mechanism the subject relies on cannot exist in a subshell (example: `reconcile/lib.sh` exports its memo only at `BASH_SUBSHELL` 0). The reason is written next to the cell, in the grammar BL-879 reads.
8. The fixture's row is deleted from `scripts/fixture-inprocess-allowlist.txt` in the same change, and BL-879's check passes on it.

The conversion of review-reroute therefore reaches these starts through item 3's uncounted route, not through C1.

No timing runs and no per-fixture before/after measurement are taken (operator ruling, 2026-10-30).

## BL-880 — the validators the pre-suite runs on every push re-run their own self-probes and setup on every invocation

**PERFORMANCE.** Operator, on the pre-push pre-suite: "Add it to the scope." The skip half, every pre-suite step skipping on a recorded pass when its inputs are unchanged by reusing the fixture key machinery, is BL-870 part B, shipped in 902d6f7e0; this entry does not restate it.

Owed here: the validators the pre-suite runs are made faster themselves, whether or not their step skips. The shape is BL-877's, self-probes re-run on every invocation. Measured at b247-tooling tip afcf1ec3a with per-arm counter markers in backlog-real-ledger: each `validate-backlog-receipts.sh` invocation re-runs its R1 and R7/R8 self-probes, about 107 starts against 3-4 corpus starts, and the fixture invokes the validator 4 times, so about 428 of its 462 starts are probes (about 321 of 342 at base). `scripts/validate-backlog-receipts.sh` is one instance: on every invocation it re-runs all of its self-probes, and starts a worker plus a `git worktree add --detach` checkout per receipt (superseded: instance 1's remedy is refuted, see the b250 instance-1 paragraph below; the entry stays PARTIAL on the general subject). For each pre-suite validator, the probe work and per-item setup are reduced without removing a probe or adding a switch that skips one: every arm self-probes by rule, and a skip switch is an opt-out. Each change keeps its validator's self-probe firing in both directions, proven by the validator's own fixture.

**Progress (BL-880, the receipts validator's keyed probe replay).** Operator ruling, 2026-10-31: "Allow the keyed replay". For this keyed form the ruling reverses this entry's own sentence "a skip switch is an opt-out". `scripts/validate-backlog-receipts.sh` replays R1 and R7/R8, and only those, when its caller exports `BR_PROBE_RECORD`, and only from a record whose content equals the run's manifest. The manifest covers the validator's bytes, reconcile/lib.sh and scripts/backlog-receipt-lib.sh, the bash and git versions, the tool paths, git's global and system config, the euid, the umask and JOBS. The parent writes the record after R7/R8 return green. R0, E0 and R9 always run. There is no default store, and the pre-push gate never sets one: `.githooks/pre-push` runs `unset BR_PROBE_RECORD` at top level before its first step, so an exported store never reaches the "backlog receipts" step. The validator itself does not unset it. Backlog-real-ledger arm p3 requires that unset at top level above the first `step`/`pk_step` call, and requires 0 setting sites over both hooks and the 32 scripts they name. Each caller's own `$TMP` is its store. A replay also leaves an inherited `BR_PROBE_COUNTER` alone, because `vbr_sec_env` unsets it outside worker mode, and the key is hashed with `git hash-object --no-filters`. Counted starts by the owner with the hook's counter, all rc=0, before at 0e190a93c (0.983.0) and after on the release tip (v0.985.0): backlog-real-ledger 462 to 248 (validator 167 to 91); backlog-receipt-binding 6937 to 5416 (validator 2532 to 2067), with the nine pr-m-* mutants and the probe-record cells counted in; backlog-size-ceiling 61, unchanged (not a caller). The pre-push step's own run is unchanged, because it fires every probe. The enforcement-map-sites instance below is open.

**Progress (BL-880, batch b250: instance 2 is a DEAD-PREMISE).** The sentence below does not describe the tree. `vrun` in `core/fixtures/enforcement-map-sites/run.sh` has passed `--arms "$id"` since d4fd318cb (`:526`); that commit's parent has no such call. The "139" was written at 178a782d0 (2026-10-30) and is the family total, not the count of full runs: at that commit the three `invocations.decl` rows for `scripts/validate-enforcement-map.sh` read `enforcement-map-sites` 61, `enforcement-map-sites-b` 34 and `enforcement-map-sites-c` 43, which sum to 138, and all three were added in 233794d68 (2026-10-28) and are unchanged since. Those rows count the validator's starts per cell, and almost all of them are `--arms` runs. The full validator runs at three sites in `core/fixtures/enforcement-map-sites/run.sh`, not two: `:551` (the sanity run), `:3134` (the `cwd_full_bit` full-mode run) and `:537` (the `full.out` run under `MX_MODE=matrix`). Instance 1, the per-receipt `git worktree add` at `scripts/validate-backlog-receipts.sh:510`, stays owed, and this entry stays PARTIAL. (Superseded for instance 1: its remedy is refuted on measurement in the b250 instance-1 paragraph below; the entry stays PARTIAL on the general subject.)

**Progress (BL-880, batch b250, 2026-11-05: instance 1's remedy is refuted on measurement).** The premise of instance 1, one `worktree add` per receipt at `:510`, is true; the remedy is what failed. Instance 1 proposed reusing the per-receipt `git worktree add --detach` in `scripts/validate-backlog-receipts.sh` (worker `mktree`, `:505-524`; the add is at `:510`). Measured by process counts only, with no timing, twice by independent hands at a797fd022, running the validator with the pre-push hook's arguments and `JOBS=8` and counting git processes with `GIT_TRACE2_EVENT` (control: 3 commands gave 3 processes). One `mktree` use today is `worktree add --detach` (which spawns a child `reset --hard`), `status --porcelain` and `worktree remove --force`: 3 execs and 4 git processes. The run made 71 worktree adds (69 from `mktree`) and 482 git processes. Only 27 tree uses are reusable, because 69 `mktree` calls ran over 42 workers in 5 xargs passes. The specified reuse (`checkout --detach` plus `reset --hard` plus `clean -fdx` plus `status`) costs 4 processes per reuse and saves 0. The cheapest variant (`checkout -f` plus `clean -ffdxq` plus `status`) saves 27, from 482 to 455 (5.6%). The live ledger has 45 entries and 2 sh receipts, each using one tree, so reuse saves 0 there; all the reuse potential is in the self-probes. The reset-based reuse (`reset --hard` or `read-tree`) also breaks the self-probe's fresh-tree properties: it never fires `post-checkout`, so the probe's untracked driver and the 444 mode of `locked.txt` (written by that hook, `scripts/validate-backlog-receipts.sh:1853-1859`) are not restored, and `clean -fdx` (a single `f`) leaves a nested repo. The cheapest variant (`checkout -f` plus `clean -ffdxq`) removes nested repos, so it is rejected on its saving alone: 27 of 482 git processes, 5.6%, and 0 on the live ledger. The cause is that `worktree add` is essentially registration plus `reset --hard`, so a reset-based reuse repeats the same work. NOTE (not built): a cheaper design that is not reuse exists. Drop the per-tree `worktree remove`, delete the directories, and run one `git worktree prune` at parent cleanup, projected at 482 to about 412 git processes (14.5%). It is not built because `prune` acts on the whole repository's worktree registry, which at the gate is the shared main checkout with its peer worktrees, and the saving's value is unmeasured. This entry stays LIVE and PARTIAL. Both named instances are struck, but the entry's subject is general: the validators the pre-suite runs on every push re-run their own self-probes and setup on every invocation. `.githooks/pre-push` routes every pre-suite step through `pk_step` (the dispatch is at `:574`), and it has 28 `step "` call lines (`grep -c '^ *step "' .githooks/pre-push`), of which 23 come before `PRESUITE_DONE=1` (`:3378`) and go through `pk_step` (`step()` calls `pk_step` only while `PRESUITE_DONE` is 0, at `:574`); this entry has not enumerated or measured the others. The b247 keyed-replay paragraph above records that the pre-push step's own run still fires every probe. What stays owed: the pre-push step's own probe replay, and the unenumerated pre-suite validators. The `git worktree prune` design above stays recorded here as a candidate.

Superseded by the DEAD-PREMISE correction above: the 139 is the family total of starts, not a count of full runs, and `vrun` has passed `--arms` since d4fd318cb. **Second instance: one validator arm per cell instead of the whole validator.** ~~enforcement-map-sites still runs `scripts/validate-enforcement-map.sh` 139 times per push.~~ Each cell decides one invariant, so `--arms <id>` would run that invariant's arm alone. This is step 6 of `docs/plans/pre-push-wall-clock.md`; `BL-274` there limits it, because an indented arm id runs its whole enclosing unit.

## BL-909 — keyword-free mutant-kill verdict lines outside BL-828's 22 delisted fixtures are invisible to M1

**DEFECT.** `scripts/validate-mutant-kills.sh` M1 refuses an unrouted kill only on a line carrying a KILLED/killed token, so a mutant verdict worded without one is never asked to go through `mutant_killed` and its base-pass flag. BL-828 item (3) routed the 15 such lines in the 22 fixtures delisted by 78966dd63; the same residue exists across the rest of the corpus. A loose grammar (non-comment `ok`/`note` lines that name a mutant or mutation, without `mutant_killed` and without KILLED/killed) hits about 669 lines across 154 fixtures, a figure from the b249-c4 contract derivation; its false-positive set is unmeasured. Examples at afea572a5: postcompact-rulebook-recovery :525, :563, :583 (`mutant: a protocol without the mandate FAILS ...`) and process-rule-pins :1529, :1562 (`MUTANT slot-target`, `MUTANT bare-marker`). Owed: measure that false-positive set, enumerated, before scoping any arm.

**Progress (batch b255, release F7; records and data only, no code).** The population is re-derived at cb6e0ab80 with the loose grammar above, committed as `docs/analysis/bl909-loose-grammar.awk` with its output `docs/analysis/bl909-loose-pop-cb6e0ab80.txt` (the run line is in the grammar file's header): non-comment lines of `core/fixtures/*/run.sh` (265 files) carrying `ok` or `note` before a quote, naming a mutant or mutation, with neither `mutant_killed` nor KILLED/killed. It holds 747 lines in 169 fixtures, against the 669 / 154 recorded. A line classifier splits them four ways. N is 108 lines that are not `ok` verdicts (`note` lines, mostly DID NOT APPLY reports). C is 227 control or apply-check lines (unmutated, baseline, anchor, applied, cmp). T is 111 twin, matrix or confinement lines (stays green, intact, only its own). K is the remaining 301, read as kill verdicts. A 30-line hand sample of K read about 83% true kills, so roughly 250 true keyword-free kills in 82 fixtures, and 47 of those 82 never call `mutant_killed` in code (control in the same run: review-shard-merge does). The classes, the sample and the precision are the b255 diagnosis hand's; this release re-derived the 747/169 population (byte-identical to the diagnosis hand's), re-counted the class files, and re-derived the 82/47 split against the cb6e0ab80 tree.

A line-level arm cannot ship. Its false-positive set is about 17% of K plus any C/T line the classifier's word lists miss, and that set cannot be enumerated, because a kill is distinguished from a control by meaning, not by text. The buildable shape is fixture-level. `scripts/validate-mutant-kills.sh --derive` would derive the set of fixtures carrying a K-class line that does not route through `mutant_killed`, and hold it as a down-only list, with the C/T/N word lists as an enumerated deny-mask. Seeds owed before building it: a fixture with a keyword-free kill line and no `mutant_killed`, absent from the list, which must FIRE; the same line routed through `mutant_killed`, which must stay quiet; a C-class control line (`unmutated copy passes`), which the deny-mask must acquit; a K line carrying a deny-mask word in a kill sense (`mutant ... intact` read as a kill), which proves the mask's reach is stated and does not cover the arm's own subject; a listed fixture that has since routed every kill, which must read STALE; and an added row absent at origin/main, which must be refused. BL-909 stays LIVE/PARTIAL.

There is no graph pipeline step behind this entry: it is a coverage gap in M1, and the two `docs/analysis/` files above are its population as data, not a reading of a consumer sprint; the cost lives in scripts/validate-mutant-kills.sh, whose M1 cannot see a keyword-free kill line.

**Progress (batch b255, release F12).** `scripts/validate-mutant-kills.sh` gains M8, M9 and M10 and a derived, count-bearing, down-only list `scripts/mutant-verdicts-allowlist.txt`, one row per fixture, `<fixture><TAB><K-line count>`, rows exactly `--derive-m8`. M8 refuses a fixture whose count of keyword-free mutant verdict lines (K lines, grammar M8_FN, built on F7's `docs/analysis/bl909-loose-grammar.awk`) exceeds its row, an unlisted fixture being 0, and prints every K line with the remedy: route a kill through `mutant_killed`, or name a non-kill sense in the first 40 characters of the message (unmutated, control, baseline, twin, stays green, intact, matrix, did not apply, applied, pairing, cmp -s). M9 refuses a stale row, a row for a fixture with no K line or no run.sh, a duplicate and a malformed row. M10 refuses a row not on origin/main's list or above its count there, so the list only goes down. The list carries a count and not membership because a membership list would acquit every NEW keyword-free kill in each listed fixture, about a third of the corpus, forever; the residual is a K line reworded or moved within a fixture at constant count, which is not seen. M8_FN reads the `ok` opener and, by the lead's ruling, the `echo "ok` and `printf` ok-format openers that F7's census did not. `scripts/validate-fixture-hygiene.sh` H5 reads the list as an added source (MVA).

Measured at aa4aea836 with M8_FN extracted and run over every run.sh: 448 K lines in 114 fixtures, 447 in 113 once relabel-theirs-collision:90 is reworded to its control sense; the committed list is 113 rows summing to 447. The deny mask acquits 286 pre-mask lines, 278 NONKILL and 8 KILL by the reader's classification (`docs/analysis/bl909-ok-mutant-lines-classified-cb6e0ab80.tsv`): check-24-adversarial-convergence:887, ledger-reverify:1480, readset-skip:3507 and :3526, retro-branch-behind-main:589, :597, :863 and :871. The first-derive check on the tip joins the 447 K lines to that file, 429 by (path, line) and 17 by (path, text) where line numbers moved: 346 KILL, 100 NONKILL (applied 24, aggregate 21, pairing 12, other 12, control 10, twin 8, baseline 5, prose 5, matrix 3), and 1 unclassified, review-shard-merge:1623, an aggregate that the reader's file lacks because its `mutant_killed` sits inside the message string. 69 listed rows carry at least one KILL line and 44 carry NONKILL lines only. A seeded fixture and line outside the file joined to nothing, in the same run.

Residual emitter classes outside every opener, non-comment run.sh lines at aa4aea836 naming a mutant word with no KILLED/killed, not K, with the reporter at command position: `live` 15, `vr_score` 7, `check` 7, `rp_score` 4, `eq` 3, `ok_` 3, `yes` 1, and a mutant word only in a second `ok` argument 6 (backlog-ledger:397).

The entry stays PARTIAL. Owed: routing the listed true kills through `mutant_killed`, measured by the list's KILL rows (69 rows, 346 lines at the tip), so the list reaches empty; the residual emitter classes above; mutant-id-only messages (`ok "m3: arm A2 fires"`); and kills emitted from a sourced helper, out of population as in M1.

There is no graph pipeline step behind this entry: it is a coverage gap in M1 and a new arm of the same validator, and the two `docs/analysis/` files above are its population as data, not a reading of a consumer sprint; the cost lives in scripts/validate-mutant-kills.sh, which gains 14 forks (105 to 119) and adds no per-line work.

## BL-914 — one `log stream` drop notice disables the pre-suite step key record for every later step in the push

**DEFECT.** `.githooks/pre-push:244` starts one `/usr/bin/log stream` per push (from `pk_trace_init`, `:226`), and the pre-suite step key skip (`pk_step`, `:266`, introduced in 902d6f7e0, 0.971.0) depends on it. At `:301` `pk_step` greps `dropped` over the whole push-long stream file `$PK_W/stream`, and `:302-303` delete the step's record and print `not recorded: the sandbox report stream dropped reports`. Observed 2026-11-05T03:29Z: session ai-dlc-d3's pre-suite prefix run printed that message for 22 of 23 steps. Mechanism: the stream is one file for the whole push, so a single drop notice, once printed, makes the grep non-zero for every later step, which fits 22 of 23. Consequence: fail-safe, because a step that is not recorded re-runs and never falsely skips, but how much the skip saves varies with box load, which is a performance-variance defect in a shipped mechanism. Related: BL-868 (a fixture verdict or count depending on load; here the verdict is fail-safe, so this is related and not an instance).

Design hazard for any fix: a per-step window is not obviously safe, because a drop notice printed after a step's end sentinel would record that step with reports missing, and a later push would skip it falsely. Any fix must pair with a test that a forced drop does not record, and a `.githooks/` edit is pushed from the main checkout by the lead. No fix in this release.

**PARTIAL.** The fix deletes the `log stream` tracer and its sentinel, profile and `sandbox-exec` wrapper outright, so no drop notice can exist to disable the record. Each step record is now `#format k2`, keyed on a hash of the raw present set (the walk `/usr/bin/find . -path ./.git -prune -o -print0`, NUL-separated, as C-sorted `<rel path>\t<f|d|l|o>\t<readlink target>` lines with backslash, TAB and newline escaped in the path and the target by `esc()`, no size and no mtime) and named `<slug>.<first 16 hex of that hash>.key`, with the full hash in a non-`#` `@present` row that `check()` compares. The new hook never reads a k1 `<slug>.key`. The present key is computed over a NUL-separated walk with an injective encoding, and it is turned off on any find error. `pk_init` writes `$PK_W/start` immediately before the present walk, and after each step the hook runs `find . -path ./.git -prune -o -newer "$PK_W/start" -print`, cumulatively from the walk and no longer per step. A change since the walk leaves that step unrecorded, and so every later step on the push, with the message `not recorded: the work tree changed since the present walk (<up to 3 paths>); no later step is skipped or recorded on this push`. When any record was written, `pk_finish` walks again, and if the present key moved or the re-walk failed it deletes every record this push wrote, printing `pre-suite keys: a path was created, deleted or retyped during the pre-suite steps (present key <16 hex> at the start, <20 hex or "(the re-walk failed)"> at the end) -- every record this push wrote is deleted: <count>`. The residual false skip this leaves, a path created and removed during the prefix by a writer that restores its parent directory's mtime both times, is BL-919. The evidence for deleting the stream rather than repairing it: the contract adversary measured that sandbox reports are not persisted (`log show` returned 0 where the live stream returned 1959, against a `logger` control that returned 1), that an SBPL trace wrote 0 files, and that every heavy window drops, about 4200 of 12000 reports delivered per round. `core/scripts/derive-fixture-readsets.sh:1990` records the same property in the deriver's own header: a `cp -R` recorded 946 of the 956 paths it read with zero drop notices, so a clean stream is not a complete one and the old drop-notice guard could never have been a completeness control.

Close condition: BL-914 stays open, with `verify: manual` and no LANDED line, and closes on two pushes from worktrees that share one git common dir: the first push records, and a later one prints `N of 23 step(s) skipped on a recorded pass` with N>=1 (a prefix run in a standalone clone seeds nothing, because the clone has its own .git), quoted in an `Outcome measured:` line on this entry. The close needs two pushes of the same sha from clean worktrees with identical present sets; a main-checkout push followed by a worktree push reads 0 skipped by design.

Yield limit: pushes from the main checkout will rarely skip, because the raw walk there covers 158091 paths (taken on the main checkout `/Users/n8/git/ai-dlc` by the contract adversary on 2026-11-05) including the agent worktrees and `.DS_Store` churn, so the present set moves between pushes. The unrecordable window now runs from `pk_init` to the last step: on the main checkout, any write under the tree during the whole prefix (a `.DS_Store`, a peer's write) leaves the rest of that push unrecorded. The newest 4 records per step are kept across every worktree sharing the common dir, so five or more worktrees with differing present sets evict each other's records. Pruning nested repos and worktrees from the walk was ruled out, because it would be an exemption from the key; whether to take it is the operator's call.

verify: manual

## BL-919 — a path created and removed during the pre-suite prefix, with its parent directory's mtime restored both times, can produce a false step skip

**DEFECT.** Residual of BL-914. A path created and then removed during the pre-suite prefix by a writer that restores its parent directory's mtime both times (as `rsync -a` or `tar x` can) is seen by neither the start-mark `-newer` check after each step nor the `pk_finish` re-walk, because it is absent from both walks and moved no mtime. A step that ran while it existed is recorded keyed on a tree without it, and a later push can skip that step. Measured by the delta adversary: push 2 skipped a step that would have failed, while the control with the path left present was caught and its records deleted. ctime cannot close it, because the hook's own atime reset (`touch -a -h`, `pk_reset_all`) moves every path's ctime. No deterministic unprivileged channel is known: sandbox reports are not persisted and an SBPL trace writes nothing, per the evidence in BL-914. No fix and no test in this release.

verify: manual

## BL-915 — the suite carries tests written only to close a backlog entry, and the owner rules require one for every close

**DEFECT.** Operator, batch b250, after release `close` grew a fixture, a receipt, four mutants and a log stub to close BL-870, an entry whose close condition was evidence from a real push: "I DON'T WANT A TEST WRITTEN JUST TO CLOSE A BACKLOG ENTRY." And: "how many other meaningless, tactical fixtures/tests do we have in our test suite that provide ZERO value but are being forced upon me??"

Measured at `d44b83ca7`, in the main checkout:
- 261 fixtures (`ls core/fixtures/*/run.sh | wc -l`).
- 96 files named `core/fixtures/*/receipt-bl<N>.sh`, in 41 fixtures (control: 515 `*.sh` files under `core/fixtures/*/`).
- 87 of the 96 belong to an entry that is no longer live (no `^## BL-<N>` heading in `docs/backlog.md`).
- Of those 87, 12 are invoked on a non-comment line of their fixture's `run.sh`, and 75 are invoked by nothing. `git grep -lF receipt-bl` over `scripts/`, `.githooks/` and `core/scripts/` names no caller of a fixture receipt (it names four `scripts/receipt-bl*.sh` files and two scripts that mention the token in prose). Control: 3 of the 9 live-entry receipts are invoked by their `run.sh`.
- Receipts are the countable part only. Arms and whole fixtures added to close an entry carry no naming convention, so they are found by reading each against the behaviour it guards.

Cause: the owner-rules block in `docs/plans/graph-ledger-full-drain.md` (action 0), Flow step 3, requires of every entry "its own fixture arm and mutant, and a replaced `verify:` receipt". It applies that to every close, including a close that records evidence and changes no behaviour.

Fix, required:
1. **Audit every fixture and arm.** A fixture or arm stays only if (a) it guards behaviour that still ships, (b) it kills at least one mutant no other fixture kills, and (c) its verdict does not depend on load (BL-868). Anything failing one of the three is removed, with no recovery copy and no list put to the operator. That includes every unreferenced receipt, every receipt whose only job was to close its entry, and any arm whose subject is gone.
2. **Rules that prevent recurrence, each with a named carrier:**
   - A records-only close (one that changes no shipped behaviour) adds no test code. A fixture arm is owed only for a behaviour change. Rewrite Flow step 3 of the owner-rules block to match.
   - A test of a mechanism lives in a fixture keyed on that mechanism's files, never in one that re-keys on `docs/backlog.md`.
   - Each rule gets a mechanical enforcer where one is constructible. A receipt file no `run.sh` invokes fails the push; the false-positive set is measured before that arm ships. Where no enforcer exists, say so in the entry.
   - The durable rule channel is at its A6 ceiling (74238/74240 at b249 close), so a rule goes in the owner-rules block, a `paths:`-scoped rule under `.claude/rules/`, or a validator arm, never in `CLAUDE.md` or an unconditional rulebook without first freeing bytes.

Close condition: the audit's removals are landed on `origin/main`, the rules above are landed with their carriers, and the suite's fixture count and receipt count before and after are recorded here with their derivations. This entry closes by its own landing. It needs no `Outcome measured:` line and no new test.

Provenance: batch b250 operator instruction; lead ai-dlc-be; owner ai-dlc-58.

**Progress (batch b250, release <placeholder: VERSION>).**

## BL-974 — a record-format error in a shard or repair part is found by the lead's join, so one malformed line costs a resume round trip

**DEFECT (efficiency).** An adversary or remediator seals its part, the lead runs the merge or join, the join refuses the part on one line (an `artifact_sha:` stem in the provenance block, an `edit:` line that does not resolve, a coverage gap in the part record), and the lead resumes the finished agent to correct that line. Population for every figure: graph's Claude Code transcripts, 151 lead and 6,018 subagent, read only, window end 2026-11-11T10:30:00Z, extracted by `docs/analysis/pipeline-efficiency-s317-s320/scripts/extract.py` with the s321/s322 edge at 2026-11-09T00:00:00Z; s322 is partial (planning complete, 3 of 8 stories done). In-session time only; handoff gaps are excluded by ruling. Resume messages to finished subagents, classified by their text: s321 13 join or merge refusals with 4.5 agent-hours of idle before the resume (21 minutes each); s322 16 with 11.2 agent-hours (42 minutes each). Each loop is also a lead turn to diagnose, a message, and a second join. Dollars: about $30 across the 29 loops, estimated at about $1 a resume; not measured per loop. The idle is agent idle, an upper bound on wall clock, because other agents may run meanwhile. Instances: s322 `requirements-p2/6.md` (`artifact_sha:` stem), s322 `requirements-repair-p1/1.md` (`edit:` line of F-1), s321 `stories-party-repair-p1/15.md` (coverage gap at line 112).

Fix direction, not built: the writer runs the join's own per-part check before it seals, so the refusal reaches the agent while it still holds the context. `--check-part` exists and the s322 agents ran it clean before the join refused them, so the gap is the difference between what `--check-part` checks and what the join and merge refuse. Close that difference in the per-part check; do not add a second checker. False-positive set and consumer cost unmeasured.

Baseline: s321
Step: requirements
Expected movement: join and merge refusals that end in a resume of the writing agent fall from 13 (s321) and 16 (s322 partial) a sprint to 2 or fewer, and no part that passed its own per-part check is refused by the join for a format reason.

Decision rule: the step-value ruling, "A step with no measured value goes" (`.claude/rules/operator-rulings.md`). Related: BL-804 (the `derivations: not replayed` refusal, one member of this class), BL-532, BL-544.

Receipt: `verify: manual`; closes on an `Outcome measured:` line counting refusal resumes on a graph sprint. What else would satisfy a weaker receipt: a fixture showing `--check-part` refuses one of the three named lines passes while the other refusal classes stay join-only. What this does not establish: which refusal classes exist beyond the three instances read; the 29 were counted by message text and not enumerated by class.

Provenance: batch 255 efficiency analysis (branch b255-efficiency-analysis), measured by the analysis session; id allocated by lead ai-dlc-7f.

**PARTIAL (v0.1057.0) — the seal now runs the join's own per-part check, so a format refusal reaches the writer at `--mark`; the outcome on a graph sprint is still owed.** Expected, as measured: the 29 resumes are 23 format refusals and 6 not this entry (5 BL-976 shard-dir corrections, 1 misclassified NEEDS_REWORK). Of the 23, 21 have a join refusal behind them; 2 do not (escalation closure is reported and never refused, `join-remediator-shards.sh` header "an open escalation is the lead's"; party-source coverage has no join arm), so refusing them at the seal would refuse bytes the join accepts. Of the 21, 20 are decidable at the seal and 1 is merge-only (the INVOKED_AT BOUND needs the dispatch id, whose probe row is written at SubagentStop). The reachable movement is therefore the 20, leaving 3 of the 23 per two sprints (s321+s322) that still end in a resume. `wait-for-deliverable.sh --mark` now runs `merge-adversarial-shards.sh --check-shard` on an adversary shard and `join-remediator-shards.sh --check-part` on a term-5 part, and refuses the seal (exit 64, nothing written) on the join's own REFUSED lines. Receipts: on real pre-fix bytes recovered from graph's transcripts, 20 of the 21 are refused at the seal (the miss is INVOKED_AT, merge-only by design), and the corrected bytes mark clean for every class but the three lanes, whose corrected bytes graph's history does not hold; a false-positive census over graph HEAD found 0 false positives in 1115 merged adversary shards and 890 merged term-5 parts, with a real-join differential (identical refusal sets, join against `--check-part`, 4 dirs, two engines) on the parts the census refused. Known gap: the seal SKIPs a document-mode shard whose whole provenance block is deleted (the mode decision reads the shard's own `artifact:` line); the merge's join still refuses it; closing it needs the mode resolved from a sealed sibling's `artifact:`, seeded in CS10/CS11's shape (0 of the 23 resumes, no instance in a 1425-shard census). Owed: an `Outcome measured:` line counting refusal resumes on a graph sprint.

Progress (batch 255, F6, records only, no code): a belt was proposed that would run `seat_complete_check` in the `--check-part` party branch (`core/scripts/join-remediator-shards.sh:2226-2233`). `--sources` (`:2280`) and the join (`:3347`) already run it. The belt was false-positive censused on a `file://` clone of graph at da6cadadd over 272 party-repair parts, s317-s322. It adds 0 new refusals in s317-s320 and s322. In s321, 53 of 68 parts go from exit 0 to exit 2, all of them through `seat_row_check`'s "no role-bound row", over 160 seats. The cause is a same-commit dating defect, and it is an existing join false positive rather than one the belt introduces: graph's squash fb6f198e7 installed `SEAT_ROW_TOKEN` and added every s321 seat in the same commit, and the shipping `--sources` and join already refuse those seats. Owed, in this order: (1) the dating fix, under which a seat added in the install commit itself counts as legacy, compared by commit sha and not by timestamp (`seat_row_check` reads the install as `%ct` at `:733`); (2) the belt, built only after (1). In `--check-part` mode the belt's `SEAT-ROWS: SKIP` lines must be prefixed `NOTE:`, because `wait-for-deliverable.sh:1006` relays only `^(WARN|NOTE):`, and the "both call" sentence at `join-remediator-shards.sh:585` must be updated. A correction to the PARTIAL paragraph's census: its 890 merged term-5 parts are all repair parts, and this branch covers only the 272 party parts among them.

**PARTIAL (v0.1068.0) — both owed items from the F6 progress paragraph are built: the seat dating fix, and the seat checks in `--check-part` in WARN form; the `Outcome measured:` line is still owed.** Item 1: a seat added by the install commit itself now counts as legacy, compared by full sha and not by timestamp. Graph's sprint squash fb6f198e7 installed `SEAT_ROW_TOKEN` and added 160 s321 seats in the same commit, so the join and `--sources` refused those seats as "no role-bound row". The rulebook had entered the sprint branch earlier, at 3a8895c, and not on main, so the false positive is reachable on main-line history only. Gap: such a round is never re-judged on main-line history. Item 2: `--check-part` runs the seat checks on a party part, and it is built in WARN form, never as a refusal, and no later hand should upgrade it. The seat judgement covers whole rounds and other agents' seat files, and the only edit that clears a refusal at seal time is deleting the part's `source:` citation, which also silences the join, because `cited` is built from `source:` lines only. The join and `--sources` still refuse. When `jq` is absent the check now SKIPs, and `--sources` on a host with no `jq` prints `SEAT-ROWS: SKIP jq-absent` and passes where it used to refuse every seat with a false "no role-bound row". Fixture arms: CPS4 (`--check-part` on a token-carrying world WARNs a row-less seat) and SR19 (a seat added in the install commit, deleted and re-added later, is judged). Measurements, a census on a `file://` clone of graph at da6cadadd over the 272 party parts of s317-s322, with `AI_DLC_PROJECT_ROOT` set to the clone: the base `--check-part` and the fixed `--check-part` give identical per-sprint rc tables (s321 53 rc 0 and 15 rc 2); 0 new REFUSED lines; 0 "no role-bound row" lines on any part; an engine carrying item 2 only refused s321 68 of 68, naming 160 seats. `--sources` over 33 records: only `s321/architecture-party-repair-p1.md` changes, from 28 "no role-bound row" refusals to 0, and the other 32 are byte-identical. Controls: a part citing a sprint-review seat (added after the install) WARNs 130 seats at `--check-part` and `--sources` refuses them; a seat with `seat-complete:` stripped WARNs at `--check-part` and refuses at `--sources`. Cost, not measured: `--mark` on a party part now runs one `jq` pass over the spawn ledger plus the seat-row git calls. Owed: an `Outcome measured:` line counting refusal resumes on a graph sprint.

## BL-975 — a subagent killed by an API connection error is noticed about an hour later

**DEFECT (efficiency).** Nothing reports a subagent whose run has ended on an API error. Population for every figure: graph's Claude Code transcripts, 151 lead and 6,018 subagent, read only, window end 2026-11-11T10:30:00Z, extracted by `docs/analysis/pipeline-efficiency-s317-s320/scripts/extract.py` with the s321/s322 edge at 2026-11-09T00:00:00Z; s322 is partial (planning complete, 3 of 8 stories done). In-session time only; handoff gaps are excluded by ruling. On 2026-11-09 five remediators dispatched together at 17:54Z ("Repair part 1, 3, 4, 8, 9 brief") stopped within four minutes; the lead resumed them between 19:00Z and 19:08Z with "Your run terminated on an API connection error (the cause is now resolved)". Four such resume messages are in s322, with 62 to 72 minutes of idle before each (4.2 agent-hours); in one the agent's last record is an unanswered `api-request`. s321 has none. Wall clock: 1.2 h for the event, on the critical path, since the round could not join without those parts. Dollars: $0. Frequency: one event in the two sprints read. Judged on consequence: every occurrence stalls a whole round until the lead happens to look.

Fix direction, not built: the wait the lead is already armed on reports an agent that has exited without sealing its deliverable, or whose transcript has had no record for longer than a bound while a request is open, as FAILED with the agent named, instead of continuing to wait. `wait-for-deliverable.sh` already has FAILED and MISSING states; the question is what signal sets them for a dead agent. No timer-based retry is asked for.

Baseline: s322
Step: gate-validation[carry-over-evaluation]
Expected movement: a beat names a dispatched agent whose last record is an unanswered api-request within `AI_DLC_OPEN_REQUEST_SECS` (1200 s) plus one 60 s check of that request, and an agent with a harness failed notice at its next check, while the lead can take a turn. Deviation, stated plainly: the reachable bound for the open-request arm is about 21 minutes after the last send (1200 s plus one 60 s check), and that does NOT meet the entry's original "under 10 minutes" wording. The harness-failed-notice arm still fires at its next check.

Decision rule: the step-value ruling, "A step with no measured value goes" (`.claude/rules/operator-rulings.md`). Related: BL-538 (wake beats), BL-576.

Receipt: `verify: manual`; the mechanism's test is the behavioural fixture `core/fixtures/wait-beat-liveness`, which drives the wait over agents that died without their deliverable; the entry closes only on the `Outcome measured:` line its PARTIAL paragraph names. What else would satisfy a weaker receipt: a prose instruction to the lead to check on silent agents. What this does not establish: why the lead noticed after an hour and not sooner; the step token is the extractor's segment for those spawns, and their shard directory is `requirements-repair-p1`.

Provenance: batch 255 efficiency analysis (branch b255-efficiency-analysis), measured by the analysis session; id allocated by lead ai-dlc-7f.

## BL-976 — a dispatch brief can name a shard directory the merge will refuse, and finished adversaries wait for the correction

**DEFECT (efficiency).** In graph s321's architecture step the lead sent "Lead correction: the merge requires shard dir `_bmad-output/planning-artifacts/s321/shards/architecture-p...`" to adversaries that had already finished and were idle. Population for every figure: graph's Claude Code transcripts, 151 lead and 6,018 subagent, read only, window end 2026-11-11T10:30:00Z, extracted by `docs/analysis/pipeline-efficiency-s317-s320/scripts/extract.py` with the s321/s322 edge at 2026-11-09T00:00:00Z; s322 is partial (planning complete, 3 of 8 stories done). In-session time only; handoff gaps are excluded by ruling. Ten such resume gaps are in the 33 s321 adversary runs over 45 minutes, 3.0 agent-hours in all, the five longest being 58, 54, 37, 15 and 9 minutes. Wall clock: about 1 h for the event, since the pass could not merge until the shards were rewritten under the right directory. Dollars: $0 measured; each correction is a resumed agent turn. BL-535 (closed) made the carry-over step state its shard-dir stem; this is the same class in the architecture step, found at the merge and not at dispatch.

Fix direction, not built: the directory the merge will accept is derived by the program that owns it and handed to the brief, or the dispatch guard refuses a brief whose shard directory the merge would refuse. Derive it; do not add a prose reminder.

Baseline: s321
Step: architecture
Expected movement: no resume message corrects a shard directory after the shards are written; the count falls from 10 in s321 to 0.

Decision rule: the step-value ruling, "A step with no measured value goes" (`.claude/rules/operator-rulings.md`). Related: BL-535, BL-536, BL-974.

Receipt: `verify: manual`; closes on an `Outcome measured:` line counting shard-directory corrections on a graph sprint, or on a fixture in which a brief naming a refused directory is denied at dispatch. What else would satisfy a weaker receipt: stating the stem in `architecture.md`, as BL-535 did for carry-over, which leaves every other step open. What this does not establish: whether the wrong directory came from the step text or from the lead's own composition; the brief text was not read.

Provenance: batch 255 efficiency analysis (branch b255-efficiency-analysis), measured by the analysis session; id allocated by lead ai-dlc-7f.

**PARTIAL (v0.1055.0) — the dispatch guard now refuses an `adversary` brief naming a shard directory the merge will refuse, using the merge's own grammar; the outcome on a graph sprint is still owed.** THE SHARD-DIR DENY in `core/hooks/ai-dlc-dispatch-guard.sh` selects every `planning-artifacts/s<N>/shards/<dir>` an `adversary` brief names and asks `merge-adversarial-shards.sh --check-shard-dir`, which runs the merge's own placement and name functions (`merge_place`, `merge_name_derive`, `merge_name`) on the path string. A REFUSED line denies the dispatch with the merge's text; no merge reachable, an exit outside 0 and 1, or a refusal with no text fails open. This meets the fixture arm of the Receipt: fixture `dispatch-model-guard` carries 9 SHARD-DIR cells and an unmutated control, and kills 6 mutants, 5 on the guard and 1 on the merge's check mode. The deny cell names s321's `architecture-adversarial-p1`. False-positive set, measured by the shipped hook over graph's 1295 deduplicated `adversary` dispatch briefs (Claude Code transcripts, read only, window end 2026-11-11T10:30:00Z): the hook at c55af8d3f (which carries BL-819's Deliverable-line deny) decides 882 allow and 413 deny, this hook rebased onto it 874 and 421 (the same figures as at the original base 418655e78), and exactly 8 decisions change, all allow to deny, all of them s321 briefs naming `architecture-adversarial-p1`. Re-derived from those briefs: the 8 went to 7 distinct agents. The entry's count of 10 resume gaps is kept as filed. The BL-974 hand's `refusals.py` re-derivation, over the same transcripts and window, counts 5 of 29 record-format resume loops as this subject's s321 "merge requires shard dir architecture-p1" corrections. Still owed: the `Outcome measured:` line, counting shard-directory corrections on a graph sprint run on an engine carrying this release (the Expected movement, 10 to 0), and the fixture's read-set trace, a close-time duty in the main checkout.

Progress (batch 255, F6): `merge-adversarial-shards.sh --check-shard-dir` handed a whole argument beginning with `-` to BSD `basename` and `dirname` as an option. At cb6e0ab80, `--check-shard-dir -x/planning-artifacts/s1/shards/arch-p1` answered REFUSED for the wrong reason ("is not under a shards/ directory") with 165 bytes of `illegal option` on stderr, and `-n` also wrote 165 bytes of stderr. Every `basename` and `dirname` in `merge_place` and `merge_rec_paths` (`core/scripts/merge-adversarial-shards.sh:1251-1269`: 10 call sites on 9 lines) now takes `--`. BSD `basename --` and `dirname --` were checked under `env -i PATH=/usr/bin:/bin`. With the fix the `-x/` dir answers OK, `-n` answers REFUSED on its placement alone, and stderr is empty for both. Receipt: `core/fixtures/content-seal/merge-arms.sh` CS-CSD drives the merge CLI directly, because the dispatch guard's selector never hands it such a string. One `--check-shard-dir` run takes the `-x/` dir, `-n` and a plain dir, and the arm asserts each dir's stdout line and an empty stderr. Mutant CSD drops `--` from every `basename`/`dirname` that carries it. The arm guards that the copy holds no `--` site and differs from the source, and the mutant is byte-identical to the base. CSD is killed at `dashx` and `stderr`, and the plain-dir twin stays OK (run.sh `MUTANT-MATRIX CSD`). A `planning-artifacts/s1/shards/-n` cell would be vacuous, because it already answers SKIP at the base. The merge script ships, so a consumer receives this change in a pull, but it has no effect a consumer can see, because the guard's selector never hands the check a string beginning with `-`. Adjacent and not changed: `merge_locate`'s `cd "$SHARD_DIR"` (`:1245`) would read a relative dir beginning with `-` as a `cd` option on a real merge. The check's contract is the path string only, so it can answer OK for such a dir while the merge then refuses it as "not enterable". The entry stays PARTIAL, and the `Outcome measured:` line above is still owed.
