## BL-972 — a pending AskUserQuestion holds the lead, so a lane that finishes during the question waits for the answer

**DEFECT (efficiency).** Population for every figure: graph's Claude Code lead transcripts and their subagent transcripts, read only, to 2026-11-11T10:30:00Z: 247 asks over s317–s322, with sprints from the `EDGES` in `docs/analysis/pipeline-efficiency-s317-s320/scripts/extract.py`. s322 is partial. In-session time only; handoff gaps are excluded by ruling. Measured by `docs/analysis/pipeline-efficiency-s317-s322/scripts/ask_split.py` (output `docs/analysis/pipeline-efficiency-s317-s322/out/ask_split.txt`). Its per-sprint call counts and hours equal `operator_asks.py`'s for s317–s321. A wait is FINISHED-INSIDE when a subagent of the same lead session, started before the ask, wrote its last record during it. FINISHED-INSIDE minutes of all answer-wait minutes: s317 197 of 398, s318 271 of 811, s319 164 of 1113, s320 791 of 3052 (26%), s321 164 of 1576 (10%), s322 20 of 537. Those lanes are not joined or followed while the question is open. 290 task notifications were enqueued inside 77 of the 247 asks: 133 from agents, the rest from background Bash and Monitor. Every FINISHED-INSIDE ask has at least one agent notification. Yet no notification-carrying attachment or user record, and no lead assistant record, falls between any question and its answer (`DLV-INSIDE` 0, `ACT-INSIDE` 0). The script's self-probe proves both counters fire on a seeded inside record and stay at 0 on a same-message near-miss. 67 queue `remove` records do fall inside, and no record shows the lead's model receiving those before the answer. Control, asserted in the script: BL-841's 10-27 06:57Z ask scores FINISHED-INSIDE (95 min; two subagents finished inside it). Dollars: $0, a wait bills nothing.

The ceiling, stated so the entry is not read as owning the whole 26.3 h. The figure that names this defect is FINISHED-INSIDE. Most of s321's largest bucket, NONE-RUNNING, 1086 of 1576 minutes (69%), is deploy-validate asks with nothing running: 940 minutes, including the 645-minute "Router PUT" and the 264-minute "Merge #1305". These are operator actions on the serial deploy path, and no lead-side mechanism shortens an operator's answer. FINISHED-INSIDE also UNDERCOUNTS, in two ways. An agent that finished inside a wait and was resumed later has its last record after the ask: 3 asks outside FINISHED-INSIDE (397 min, all sprints) carry an enqueued agent `finished` notification. And background Bash and Monitor completions held by an ask are not bucketed at all. A lead that keeps working while a question is open could also dispatch ready lanes inside a wait of any bucket, and no transcript says where those were.

**Correction (2026-11-11, b255 F4b).** `ask_split.py` now deduplicates asks by `tool_use` id, so one s321 ask (23.9 min) that sits in two lead files is counted once. Under the deduplicated script the population above reads 246 asks, and s321 reads 1552 min with NONE-RUNNING 1063 and deploy-validate 916, against 247, 1576, 1086 and 940. FINISHED-INSIDE 164 is unchanged in minutes, and its share reads 164 of 1552 (11%) where the s321 figure above says 164 of 1576 (10%); NONE-RUNNING reads 1063 of 1552 (68%) where the ceiling paragraph says 1086 of 1576 (69%); the Expected movement below therefore reads against 164 of 1552. The receipt's s321 ask-count baseline is `ask_split.py`'s deduplicated 56, not `operator_asks.py`'s 57. The two tools differ by that one duplicated row, so a closer must compare the ask count only within `ask_split.py`'s output, never across the two tools, and a count of 56 against 57 is not a fall in asks.

Fix direction, not built. An operator ruling on contact shape is owed first. While a question is unanswered, the lead still receives task notifications, and it joins and dispatches lanes the question does not hold. The question itself goes out at once, in `AskUserQuestion`, unchanged. Asking in prose instead of the tool is NOT this fix: Rule 11(a) requires `AskUserQuestion` for an ambiguity question, and `core/hooks/ai-dlc-continue.sh:1637` records the measured failure of the prose form (the operator: "you haven't asked any question ... buried in the transcript"). No variant may suppress, batch away, defer or hold back a question, or lower the ask count. Whether the harness offers a non-blocking ask is unmeasured.

Baseline: s321
Step: gate-validation[implementation]
Expected movement: on asks with a notification enqueued inside, `ACT-INSIDE` (`ask_split.py`, the per-sprint "ACT-INSIDE on those asks" figure) rises from 0 on s321 (60 notifications over 16 asks, none acted on before the answer). FINISHED-INSIDE minutes held by an open question fall from 164 of 1576. The ask count does not fall.

Decision rule: the step-value ruling, "A step with no measured value goes" (`.claude/rules/operator-rulings.md`). The step judged here is the lead's idle hold, never an ask: every question still goes out. Related: BL-841.

Receipt: `verify: manual`. It closes on an `Outcome measured:` line from a graph sprint read by `ask_split.py`, giving "ACT-INSIDE on those asks" above 0, with the ask count and FINISHED-INSIDE minutes against s321's 164 beside it. A fall in the ask count against the baseline sprint is a contact regression: it is never credited, and it blocks the close whatever else moved. What else would satisfy a weaker receipt: a lead that holds a question back until its lanes drain. That turns every FINISHED-INSIDE wait into NONE-RUNNING and moves the minutes to 0 with the ask count intact. It is the deferral this entry forbids, and it is why the close is keyed on `ACT-INSIDE`, which a deferring lead cannot produce, and not on minutes. Fewer or faster-answered questions shrink every bucket without the mechanism, which the ask-count rule refuses. What this does not establish: whether a ready action existed undispatched at any ask. Subagents dispatched from another lead session are not joined, and asks issued from a subagent are not counted. `ACT-INSIDE` is positional: a mechanism that returned the tool result at once and delivered the answer some other way would read 0 whatever the lead did. When the harness's ask mechanism is measured, check what this column records under it before reading it. The step token is `extract.py`'s step for the asks carrying the most s321 FINISHED-INSIDE minutes (87 of 164).

Provenance: batch 254, BL-841 R1 by ruling of lead ai-dlc-7f; id allocated by ai-dlc-7f.

## BL-978 — the smoke-surface scaffold never checks whether the path it creates is ignored, so a plain `git add` silently refuses and `smoke-not-updated` never runs

**DEFECT.** Filed 2026-11-11. The reconcile scaffold in `core/skills/ai-dlc-update/reconcile/apply.sh` creates the declared smoke-surface file from its template and tells the consumer "It is YOURS from here ... Commit it between sprints" (the RESOLVED text around line 2795). It never runs `git check-ignore` on the scaffolded path (`check-ignore` occurs 0 times in `apply.sh`). In a consumer whose `.gitignore` covers that path, a plain `git add` refuses silently, the file stays untracked, and `smoke-not-updated`, which reads the tracked surface, never runs. The instruction to commit it is followed correctly and does nothing.

Provenance is graph's state at da6cadadd468, read only. `docs/smoke-surface.txt` is on disk with 26 non-empty lines. `git cat-file -e HEAD:docs/smoke-surface.txt` exits 128, against a control exit of 0 for the committed `scripts/test-aws-smoke.sh`. `git check-ignore -v` names `.gitignore:29:*.txt`. `git log --all` on the path finds 0 commits. So the file has existed on disk and has never been committed on any ref. BL-845's `WARN smoke-surface-ignored` surfaces this at every handoff once graph has it, but only after the handoff; the reconcile that created the file is silent.

Owed: at reconcile, when the declared surface path is ignored and not tracked, print the `git add -f <path>` remedy beside the scaffold's RESOLVED text.

BOOTSTRAPPING file (`apply.sh`): it ships alone per plan action 2 (`docs/plans/graph-ledger-full-drain.md:1407`, "a candidate whose fix touches a BOOTSTRAPPING file ... ships ALONE"). Evidence on the mechanics, which may make the plan rule relaxable for a step-7-only change like this one; that is the operator's call, to be raised at batch close. The scaffold arm is in `apply.sh`, which runs at step 7 (the gated apply), not in steps 1-2. `apply.sh` is in the machinery slice (`core/skills/ai-dlc-update/**` in `reconcile/setup-sites.md`'s `machinery:` list), which step 2 writes by hand on `SELF-UPDATE-OK` and then re-invokes the skill, so step 7 of the DELIVERING pull runs the new `apply.sh` and the fix acts on that pull. Only when step 2 defers (`SELF-UPDATE-DEFER`, `SELF-UPDATE-UNDECIDED`, an un-synced branch, or a refused self-update push) does step 7 run the installed copy, and then the fix acts from the next pull; a release carrying other work changes neither path. Scope note: the arm runs only when the declared path is ABSENT (`[ ! -f "$CONSUMER/$SS_REL" ]`), so a consumer whose surface is already on disk and ignored, graph's state, never reaches it; that state is BL-845's `WARN smoke-surface-ignored`.

Receipt: `verify: manual`. The receipt shape is a reconcile fixture arm that seeds a consumer whose `.gitignore` covers the surface path, runs the scaffold, and asserts the `git add -f` remedy line. No runner-wired receipt exists without a fixture change, and a `has` on `apply.sh` for `check-ignore` is closable by a comment.

Provenance: batch 255, cluster F3-smoke-surface; id allocated by the lead.

verify: manual
